The idea of dropping a power-draining, water-gulping ten-building campus on a place as small as Sutton is exactly why data centers have become the hot-button local issue all over the country. It is sheer oligarchical bullying over which the country has raised its hackles. This would be the fourth-largest data center in the United States. And, as WGBH points out, some sports oligarchs are leading the parade on these projects, which is reason enough to give every one of these projects a serious side-eye.

It’s good to be rich in this country. You may not have noticed.

Elsewhere, Erik Loomis of LG&M points us to another oligarch’s dreamscape, this one in the beleaguered state of West Virginia. From The Washington Post:

Pause for a moment and roll that business-school candy corn around in your mouth for a second. A Nuclear Lifecycle Innovation Campus. A glow-in-the-dark euphemism for the good people of Jefferson County.

We’re back in the three-card-monte game of “Jerbs!” that we all had to play in relation to our old friend the Keystone XL pipeline. We have to be careful how many of these jobs are temporary construction jobs and how many are the strippers who come to town to entertain them.

(Jesus, Louisiana? Really? Are you prepared to move aside one of your eleventy-million chemical plants and refineries to make room for nuclear-waste disposal facilities? Take a knee on this one, okay?)

You mean communities are anxious about becoming repositories for poisonous waste with a half-life of up to 24,000 years? What is wrong with people anyway?

Energetic local community activism on issues like these was one of the most underrated sources of energy behind the historic Democratic sweep two weeks ago. People are starting to enjoy fighting the power again.

In that spirit, we conclude, as is our custom, in the great state of Oklahoma, whence Blog Official Overseas Operative Friedman of the Algarve brings us yet another story about toxic waste. From Tulsa Flyer:

Delightful. And who was it that tried to kill Bell Cow Lake?

It is going to take decades to get clear of all the damage these clowns have done, if we even can, which I doubt more seriously every day.

This is your democracy, America. Cherish it.

#7011. Prince Harry Shocks Viewers With ‘This Morning’ Appearance—Here’s What He Revealed

Prince Harry has surprised This Morning viewers with an extra-long appearance on the most famous sofa in Britain. The Duke of Sussex appeared alongside regular Friday hosts Dermot O’Leary and Alison Hammond and joined them for a few segments throughout the morning show.

Over the course of his appearance on the ITV chat show, Harry—who was there to discuss his work with the Invictus Games—revealed more insight into his children Lilibet and Archie’s new life in the UK and gave a hint at his upcoming Christmas plans with Meghan and their family.

Here’s everything Prince Harry said throughout his appearance on This Morning.

Following the family’s move back to the UK in September earlier this year, Harry shared that the children, Lilibet and Archie, are finally getting British accents having spent most of their childhood so far in California, adding he was “squeezing the accents out of them.”

He also revealed there was a bit of a “culture shift” for the children when it came to their new schooling as they now have to wear school uniform, something they didn’t have to do while in school in the U.S.

“I think the kids being in school uniforms is a big change for them,” he explained. “But it has certainly helped. The confidence in both of them has gone through the roof since being in a UK school, which is really, really nice to see.”

During his chat with Alison and Dermot, the hosts quizzed him on all things UK vs. USA, and Alison asked him for his thoughts on Robert Pattinson being cast as James Bond, to which Prince Harry responded, “I think it’s in great hands.” He also added he couldn’t wait to see the film.

He was also asked whether he preferred EastEnders or Suits (which is the show his wife Meghan Markle is most known for acting in) and he ultimately picked Suits.

He revealed his choice was “Runnin’ (Lose It All)” by Naughty Boy feat. Beyoncé and Arrow Benjamin, and added it’s the song he listens to while doing a cold plunge.

The Duke of Sussex joined Dermot and Alison for the cooking segment of the series where they were learning how to create a Christmas cake with Phil Vickery.

During the segment, the hosts asked him about whether he liked to bake and he revealed that children Lilibet and Archie liked to bake, but that the whole family was a fan of The Great British Bake Off, which is of course presented by Alison.

Harry added that the series was good this year and he was a big fan of Tom to win the series.

As the cooking segment wound down, Alison asked him who would be doing the cooking at Christmas in his home this year.

He went on to say his wife and mother-in-law were the better cooks in his household and so they would be doing the cooking for the festive season. The Duke also revealed they may do things differently this year and have a Christmas gumbo, too!

#7007. Prince William and Kate Middleton’s Advisor Shares a Rare Comment About Their Relationship

A senior royal aide says the Prince and Princess of Wales are “very much a team” after their whirlwind India tour.

The Prince and Princess of Wales made headlines this week on their royal visit to India, which saw them attend the 2026 Earthshot Prize Awards ceremony, meet the country’s prime minister, drive a tuk-tuk, and even ride the metro. Now that they’re back in the UK, those in the couple’s inner circle have been reflecting on the trip, and one thing is clear: William and Kate are closer than ever.

Speaking to reporters (via the BBC), the pair’s most senior advisor described them as “very much a team” following their multiday tour in India. “It has been particularly special for them to experience this moment together,” said private secretary Ian Patrick. “They are very much a team.”

Pointing to the reason why, the royal insider explained that it’s all to do with taking part in each other’s passions. “The prince has really enjoyed having the princess alongside him for Earthshot,” the royal advisor said, referring to William’s environmental awards project. “She’s enormously proud of what he’s achieved.”

Commenting on William and Kate’s trip more broadly, the private secretary added, “The prince and princess have had a thoroughly enjoyable time in India. They’ve been incredibly touched by the warmth of the welcome they’ve received.”

Over the past few days, the Waleses have kept fans up to date with their adventures in India by sharing various photos and video clips on Instagram. In one down-to-earth video, William joined locals for a game of gully cricket in Mumbai. Another clip showed the pair wearing traditional South Asian style clothing at the Earthshot Prize Awards ceremony.

“The most beautiful and sweetest couple,” one fan commented on the video. “The most beautiful royal couple on the planet without a doubt,” agreed someone else.

#7006. Jack O’Connell on McQueen, Ink, and the “Heavy Hitters” of Sinners

He may not be James Bond, but the actor is still dressed to kill.

Jack O’Connell may not be James Bond, but he’s still dressed to kill. At the 17th annual Governors Awards, hosted by the Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences, the Sinners and 28 Years Later: The Bone Temple star rolled up in a mono-black suit sourced from McQueen’s Spring/Summer 2027 pre-collection: a tailored jacket with ultrawide lapels, black wool barathea trousers, and polished Jermyn brogues. If anyone at the awards exuded main-character energy, it was O’Connell.

“It’s a real lovely, beautiful chance to congregate and celebrate,” O’Connell tells me days later. “You come away from them feeling really inspired. There’s a lot of love in that room.”

He adds, “It is my mission now to binge Glenn Close [movies].” (The 79-year-old legend received an Academy Honorary Award for her many decades of contributions to the industry.)

But O’Connell’s all-black eveningwear, courtesy of McQueen, is award worthy on its own. No stranger to events like these, “sometimes you don’t wanna look uniform,” O’Connell says. The now grown-up James Cook from Skins opted for a double-breasted jacket with distinctly large lapels that he says were “almost a winged tip.” He felt it was something Alexander McQueen himself might have imagined. “Just a dapper bit of Lee McQueen—it’s nice, isn’t it? To push the boat out a little bit.”

He credits his stylist, Luke Day, for pointing him in the right direction. “We pick our moments. Sometimes we want to toe the line; sometimes we don’t. You don’t always feel like slapping a dicky bow tie on. It’s great. It mixes it up.”

When O’Connell isn’t mixing it up in front of flashing cameras, he likes to dress to his surroundings—as any man ought to. “If I’m in the countryside, it’s boots. If I’m in the city, it’s kicks. I really like a lot of Axel Arigatos on my feet. But Reebok was always the staple. I love a Reebok.”

The man could use a pair of Reeboks, because he’s had quite the year running ahead. In 2025, he was a most memorable menace in Ryan Coogler’s vampire scorcher Sinners. He kicked off 2026 in 28 Years Later: The Bone Temple as Jimmy Crystal, the bleach-blond cult leader of butt-kicking devil worshippers in the zombie apocalypse. He will soon star in Ink (which hits select theaters December 11 before it lands on Netflix on January 8), Danny Boyle’s screen adaptation of the stage play about the British tabloid The Sun. If it bleeds, it leads, indeed.

All the while, his name got thrown into the mix as the next James Bond. Though O’Connell had public support from friends like Boyle, he downplayed how much he was actually in the running (as in an October interview with Entertainment Weekly). Now that Robert Pattinson is 007, he doesn’t have to answer those questions anymore. “He’s one of the best in the biz, isn’t he? I love his work,” O’Connell says of R-Patz. “He’s a great set of hands. Anything he turns to, he just kinda nails. Really interesting actor, makes very unobvious choices. I’ve been a fan of his for a long time.”

Without Bond looming over him, O’Connell is free to focus on what he’s actually cooking up. Lately, he’s been hard at work on two big blockbusters for 2027: Godzilla x Kong: Supernova and A Quiet Place Part III. “It’s been one of my favorites for sure,” O’Connell says, reflecting on the past year. “Tied for going to the Oscars and watching my friends win trophies, it is up there as one of the best experiences of my life. I hope we keep pushing.”

So what can audiences expect to see in Supernova? Besides monster wrestling, of course. “We follow Kaitlyn [Dever]’s character up the ranks,” he explains. “She’s studying these monsters. Her brother’s part of a new organization. I can’t give too much away about it. And Trapper’s just being Trapper. You know how Trapper is.”

The project, with its green screens and CGI kaiju, is the biggest O’Connell has undertaken yet. But the large-scale endeavor brings him back to his childhood. “I remember kids running around the playground pretending to be Godzilla,” he says. “It’s omnipresent, isn’t it?”

He adds, “It’s not every job that I’m doing that type of thing. It was great to explore it to the maximum. You know who the main characters are. It’s not the people. It’s great supporting these pixelated legends.”

O’Connell can say even less about A Quiet Place Part III. “It’s in the aftermath of this horrific apocalyptic event,” he says. “It’s a tale of trauma, loss. It’s stuntier than any of us anticipated. But at the center of it is this very beautiful human story where we’re saying, What is the cost of all this fucking chaos and carnage, you know?”

But in some ways, O’Connell is still riding the high of Sinners. “People responded to it in such a way,” he says about the Oscar-winning thriller in retrospect. “It was great to see people unite at the cinema en masse. It’s great when one film can do that. I put it down to Ryan Coogler, our cast, and our entire team across the board. Everywhere you looked, it was packed with heavy hitters.”

Next time you catch O’Connell, bet he’s dressed in McQueen. “I really love what Alexander McQueen, as a person and as a creative, stood for,” he says. “We’re from similar backgrounds, which is rare for me in this world. It makes it meaningful. I celebrate his story, so any opportunity to work with them is a really heartfelt one.”

He swears you’ve seen nothing yet. “I don’t feel like I’ve done my best work yet. Far from it.”

Clothing by McQueen Spring Summer 2027 Pre-collection, wearing a sharp lapel tailored jacket and trousers in black wool barathea, a harness shirt in black cotton poplin with safety pin embroidery, and Jermyn brogues in black polished leather.

#7004. How the ‘blue tsunami’ crested in North Carolina

North Carolina Rep.-elect LaKesha Womack talks to reporters Sunday at the AC Hotel in Washington as she arrives for new member orientation. (Tom Williams/CQ Roll Call)

LaKesha Womack faced towering odds as an underfunded Democrat making her first run for public office in a North Carolina district that Donald Trump carried by 15 points two years ago.

But as early voting surged in mid-October, she sensed she might pull off an upset over Republican Rep. Tim Moore, the influential former speaker of the North Carolina House and a chief architect of the gerrymander that aimed to keep the 14th District safely red.

“There were a lot of pins and needles,” said Womack, a 49-year-old ordained minister who works at a Charlotte-based nonprofit. After seeing long lines at polling places extend into Election Day, she grew more confident: “People were ready to exercise their voice.”

Womack – who raised less than $60,000 to Moore’s $2.1 million – eked out a 2-point win in the district, which reaches from the Charlotte suburbs to mountain counties in the west.

Saddled with an unpopular president and broad dissatisfaction with the status quo, Republican incumbents across the nation experienced a midterm drubbing last week. As of Monday evening, House Democrats had netted 42 seats, with more races left to be called. The GOP in North Carolina, a state that Trump carried three times, was among the hardest hit.

Democrats have so far flipped five House seats in the Tar Heel State. Besides Womack, the “blue tsunami” also swept in Paul Barringer, a lawyer who founded an outdoor clothing line, and High Point Mayor Cyril Jefferson. And Army veteran Richard Ojeda, who once served in the West Virginia Senate, defeated Rep. Richard Hudson, the chairman of the National Republican Congressional Committee.

Democrats also won two races that had been seen as competitive for much of the cycle. Farmer Jamie Ager flipped an open seat in the state’s mountain west, while Rep. Don Davis held on to his Eastern North Carolina district, which Republicans had redrawn in their favor.

Meanwhile, in the state’s hilly northwest corner, 11-term Republican Virginia Foxx, the chairwoman of the powerful Rules Committee, is fighting for her political future: As of Monday afternoon, former journalist Chuck Hubbard led her by 99 votes in the 5th District, though The Associated Press had yet to call the race.

That was also true in the neighboring 10th District, where GOP incumbent Pat Harrigan had a 1-point edge over Democratic physician associate Ashley Bell. Also uncalled were a pair of contests in Eastern North Carolina, where Republican Rep. Greg Murphy clung to a 233-vote edge over Democratic former state Rep. Raymond Smith and GOP Rep. David Rouzer led Democratic social worker Kimberly Hardy by less than a point.

“Nobody could have predicted this,’’ said David Wilson Brown, who leads the Democratic Party in Gaston County near Charlotte and has lost two bids for the House, including to Foxx in 2020. “It’s a watershed moment.”

The Democratic rout in the midterm elections was powered by victories in states with new GOP-favored maps, including Texas, Ohio and Florida.

But Democrats’ success in North Carolina was especially striking given the steep structural disadvantages the party faced. Since Republicans flipped the state legislature in 2010, and with it control of the redistricting process, no House GOP incumbent had lost a general election in the Tar Heel State until last week.

This year’s  congressional map – the state's sixth in seven election cycles – was engineered by the Republican-led legislature to help the party capture 11 of the state's 14 House seats.

But the Democratic surge was so big that it easily overpowered the GOP’s built-in edge. Democratic candidates were also lifted by former Gov. Roy Cooper, whose 20-point Senate win provided the underticket with powerful coattails.

“In order for them to gerrymander our state in the way that they did, they were spread very thin,” Brown said, referring to state Republicans. “So they were kind of ripe to be defeated that way.”

The repeated rounds of redistricting may have also erased some of the advantages of incumbency, according to Peter L. Francia, a political science professor at East Carolina University. “If you carve up a district every two years, you've taken away some of that [advantage]. And I think that that came back and hurt Republicans in this cycle.”

Doug Heye, a GOP strategist and a former spokesman for the Republican National Committee, said redistricting “ticked off a whole lot of voters, and really activated the Democratic base.”

On the Republican side, voters were “less motivated because they were unexcited about Trump,’’ Heye added.

Republican incumbents accustomed to winning by comfortable margins weren’t prepared for a political environment driven by anger over the war in Iran and angst about the rising cost of living.

“Clearly, a lot of those Republicans didn’t take their elections seriously,” Heye said. “They won last time with 18 points. They weren't on anyone's radar screen, so they didn't think they had to do anything.”

Womack credits her win to a grassroots campaign centered on economic issues. While her race wasn’t on the national radar – Moore, after all, won by 16 points in 2024 – and her campaign didn’t have money for ads, she was out in the district doing events every day.

“There are a lot of working families right now who are in the missing middle,” she said in an interview. “They make too much money to get help, but they don't make enough money to get ahead. … Someone who can be working a full-time job, but still doesn’t have enough money to buy groceries or to afford their medications or to have access to health insurance.”

Womack said her own struggles have shaped her approach to politics. This week, she’s slated to appear in court on a felony charge for allegedly failing to return a rental car in 2019. The incident wound up in one of Moore’s attack ads.

“There are so many people who [understand] what happens when you're down on your luck and you don't have enough money to make good financial decisions,’’ she said. “Most people don't want those bad decisions to be a definition of their character for the rest of their life.”

Early in the midterm cycle, Democratic opposition research group American Bridge prepared detailed dossiers on Republican House incumbents in North Carolina.

“Someone like Virginia Foxx, she’s been on the Hill forever,” said Pat Dennis, the group’s president. “She was somebody … who under the radar could be vulnerable. So we started really early.”

American Bridge’s political arm spent $340,000 on ads targeting Foxx. One spot featured a man who identified himself as a Trump voter expressing anger at the government’s response to Hurricane Helene, which devastated the mountain communities of Western North Carolina two years ago.

Other Democrats scoffed at the group’s decision to spend money targeting entrenched Republicans far from the main House battleground.

“There was a lot of reluctance from people to really believe that that data was correct, but it was,’’ Dennis said. “Then over the summer, we saw her numbers collapsing, so we put paid media behind it and it really paid off.”

Hubbard ran against Foxx two years ago and lost by almost 20 points.

“I kind of had a notion I was going to do this again,’’ he said in an interview Saturday while en route to Washington for freshman orientation. “I don’t like to lose at checkers, much less a congressional race.”

After a court-drawn map resulted in a split North Carolina delegation after the 2022 elections, state Republicans won back control of the map-making process and went on to win 10 of the state’s 14 House seats under a new map two years later.

At Trump’s urging, Republicans made further tweaks to the North Carolina map last year, but the original gerrymander finally came back to bite the GOP in 2026, according to Hubbard.

“North Carolina is one of the most severely gerrymandered states,’’ he said. “And what happened was they had to steal from Peter to pay Paul. It's been referred to by political scientists as a dummymander.”

As the vote count in the 5th District continues, Foxx, whose campaign declined to comment for this story, has requested the presence of nonpartisan election observers.

They “will serve as important, watchful eyes to ensure that this sacred process is not impeded and that all legal votes are counted in a consistent, thorough manner,’’ she posted on social media.

But even before the final races are called, Republicans are already looking to the next election.

Heye, who expects North Carolina to remain a purple state in 2028 and beyond, cautioned the Democrats who won last week not to get too comfortable in Washington. He invoked Fred Heineman, the former Raleigh police chief who rode the 1994 “Republican Revolution” to Congress – and lasted a single term.

“Some of them will lose, and some of the people who lost may run two years from now and get their seat back,’’ he said.

One GOP operative, who requested anonymity to speak candidly, offered up a similar assessment.

Trump “didn't keep his promises” and that “just got voters very angry and suppressed the GOP base from coming out because, in a midterm election it's a base turnout contest,’’ the strategist said.

“But these Democrats are renting these districts for two years.”

#7003. One Martini to Rule Them All

The case for ordering a vodka martini, always.

At a dinner a few years ago, my fiancé’s mother, an elegant former Wall Street banker, ordered a vodka martini, dry, with three olives. I grew up in New Orleans, where I was weaned on sickeningly sweet, sticky cocktails, but I was always fascinated by the martini’s sophistication. I’d seen the drink in the hands of characters like Roger Sterling from Mad Men, who would expertly balance his V-shaped glass as he dropped a cynical witticism. So that night, I told the waiter to make it two. Ever since then, I have favored vodka martinis over gin, the base spirit for the by-the-books recipe.

I’m not the only one. Bartenders in New York have started to notice a trend away from gin. Chris Amirault of Bar Chimera in Manhattan says that he makes four vodka martinis for every one gin martini. “Vodka pays the bills, and it’s what a lot of people prefer,” says Brian Evans of the Lobby Bar at the Hotel Chelsea, also in Manhattan.

For those who insist that a true martini must only be made with gin, Tristan Stephenson, host of the Curious Bartender podcast, says, “History isn’t on the side of purists. The martini has been a moving target since the 1880s. It started sweeter, with maraschino, orange curaçao, and sugar syrup making appearances. Then it dried out over decades, then had the vermouth all but exorcised from it. Then came olive brine and everything since.”

Why do many martini drinkers prefer vodka? Ti Adelaide Martin, coproprietor of New Orleans staple Commander’s Palace, succinctly explains one reason: “Vodka is the more approachable spirit.” Unlike gin, whose botanicals impart noticeable flavors to a martini, vodka doesn’t overpower other ingredients. It’s more of a blank canvas, which is why it’s used for variations like the espresso martini, which is now the fifth-most-ordered drink at bars, according to Drinks International. Instead of strong flavor, vodka provides “texture, weight, and a kind of icy austerity that can be genuinely beautiful when the vodka is good and the drink is properly cold,” says Stephenson.

And, let’s be honest, we also drink vodka martinis for the booze. “Vodka tastes bracing,” says Esquire editor in chief Michael Sebastian. “I want to take a sip of my martini and get the feeling that it’s strong and it’s cold, and it goes through your whole body and you feel better and loose immediately.”

Now I order my vodka martini with a splash of vermouth, just dirty enough to make me thirsty, and shaken so that thin ice shards float on top. You might prefer yours vermouth forward, citrus focused, or dirty as swamp water. It’s all fair game. And that’s the beauty of a vodka martini: It’s a personal drink. My future mother-in-law has her order, and I have mine. What’s yours going to be?

#7002. This ‘DWTS’ Pro Accidentally Revealed He Was Told “Not to Discuss” Ezra Frech and Hailey Bills Dating Rumors

The rest of the cast clearly cannot stop teasing them.

No matter how much Ezra Frech and Hailey Bills try to avoid dating rumors, their Dancing With the Stars co-stars aren’t letting them escape.

During a TikTok livestream on the official DWTS account earlier this week, Alan Bersten and Brandon Armstrong were tasked with interviewing backstage at the ballroom—and they wasted no time in teasing Hailey about her rumored romance with Ezra.

According to screen recordings reposted by fans, Alan and Brandon jokingly cornered Hailey as she laughed and tried running off to her mark.

“The director just told us not to mention anything about relationships, so we won’t,” Alan said, poking fun at the situation before he reassured her, “What I’m saying is we won’t.”

This isn’t the first time Hailey—or Ezra—has been teased about the dating rumors during DWTS’s TikTok livestream. Earlier this season, Emma Slater and Sharna Burgess tried being Hailey’s wingwomen by bringing up the Paralympian unprompted.

“Do people want to see Ezra Frech?” Sharna asked, to which Hailey responded, “They probably do!” Emma then turned the attention to Hailey, asking, “How much do you wanna see Ezra Frech?”

“Me? I would love to see Ezra Frech!” Hailey responded between laughs, while Emma winked, “Ezra, Hailey would love to see you!”

What’s more: Sharna and Emma attempted to get some intel on Ezra’s relationship status during the same livestream, though Ezra confirmed he was “single” and “locked in on dancing.”

Sharna further pressed him, jokingly asking if he would be “interested in that changing.” He doubled down, saying, “I’m locked in on dancing right now. That’s all I’ll say.”

^^^ This all comes after a source previously told The Sun that they are allegedly dating in secret and “plan to reveal their relationship after the season ends.”

“They are being really careful not to post each other right now because they don’t want the chatter about them potentially dating to overtake everything else,” the insider said at the time.

Ezra and Hailey haven’t publicly addressed their rumored romance aside from the occasional teasing from their DWTS castmates, but honestly? Wouldn’t be mad at this pairing!!

#7001. Frog Closures Are the Unexpected Detail Defining Fall Jackets

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

It’s all in the details, from intricate knots to elaborate braiding

There are plenty of ways to make a statement with your outerwear, from an oversize silhouette to a high collar or bold color. But this fall, designers are turning their attention to a smaller detail: the fastening. Jackets featuring decorative knots, elaborate loops, and ornate toggles have emerged as one of the season’s biggest outerwear trends, offering an alternative to standard buttons and zippers.

In particular, frog fastenings are having a resurgence, bringing a fanciful finishing touch to everything from minimalist wool peacoats to tailored military-inspired jackets. Typically constructed from fabric or braided cord, the fasteners consist of a knotted button and a loop. Originally designed to secure garments, the closures have evolved into a more ornamental role with intricate shapes and floral-like designs.

While they may seem new for the season, frog-fastener jackets have a history that spans centuries. In Chinese dress, knotted closures known as pankou have long appeared on garments such as the qipao and tangzhuang. By the 18th and 19th centuries, frogging had also become a feature of European military uniforms, worn by cavalry regiments whose fitted jackets were adorned with braided cords.

Fashion has revisited the detail since then. Oscar de la Renta incorporated frog fastenings into his designs in the 1990s, while Valentino explored versions for its fall 2015 couture collection. More recently, for fall 2026, designers reinterpreted knotted closures: At Kallmeyer, black toggles punctuated a gilded jacquard jacket, while Kim Shui incorporated the detail into fur-trimmed blazers and opera jackets. Meanwhile, labels like Khaite and Ann Demeulemeester embraced military-inspired looks featuring ornamental braiding and embellished fronts.

Today’s store-rack versions range from collarless blazers with understated knots to dramatic outerwear designs featuring detailed cording. Brands like Róhe have introduced contemporary takes on frogging, while Hong Kong–based fashion label Sau Lee has reimagined traditional Tang-style jackets with modern silhouettes.

Rather than reinventing jackets entirely, designers are proving that something as minuscule as a fastening can make a familiar silhouette feel new again for the season. Ahead, shop our favorite takes on the look.

#6999. A Deep Dive Into Brittany Snow and Hunter Moreno’s Increasingly Public Relationship

Yes, Brittany finally confirmed that they’re engaged.

In case you missed it: The Hunting Wives star Brittany Snow is engaged to photographer and creative director Hunter Moreno, and if the news took you by complete surprise due to having literally no idea she was dating anyone, you’re not alone. Their relationship is extremely low-key—like, so low-key that they’ve only been photographed together on one or two occasions, and the only reason we, the people, first learned about their engagement is because Claire Danes was caught asking about it on a red carpet.

That said, there are some cute details to go over, so keep reading for a full timeline of Brittany and Hunter’s relationship!

Brittany and her ex-husband, Selling the OC star Tyler Stanaland, announce their split after two-and-a-half years of marriage in a joint statement:

“This decision was made with love and mutual respect for one another,” they wrote on Instagram (via People). “We have realized we need to take some time and make sure we are each living our most fulfilling and authentic lives. We started this journey as best friends and our relationship will continue to be a priority not only for us but for our dog Charlie.”

Four months after announcing their separation, Brittany reportedly files for divorce, citing “irreconcilable differences.”

Brittany and Tyler’s divorce is reportedly finalized five months after she filed.

Brittany makes an appearance on Call Her Daddy, where she tells host Alex Cooper, “It’s so weird for me to be single. I’m saying weirdly because it’s very easy for me to get in a relationship, and I’ve been actively trying to be alone and be with myself and just feel the feelings and not have to band-aid it with sparkly, shiny, emotional things.”

She adds that she’s been “in and out” of some flings, but has been pretty much keeping the vibes casual.

Brittany and Hunter start dating—or, at least, that’s when they’re first snapped kissing by paps while walking a dog.

Brittany tags Hunter in these pics, seemingly giving him photo cred:

Brittany shows up at the The Beast in Me premiere, and red carpet footage seemingly catches her telling Claire Danes that she’s engaged. At least based on lip readers thinking Claire says, “You’re engaged. When did that happen?!”

Not long after, TMZ confirms that, yep, Brittany and Hunter are engaged after a year of dating!

A source tells Us Weekly that “Hunter is an extraordinary guy. He’s a mental health advocate and just a salt-of-the-earth kind of guy. They are so happy together.”

Another insider says, “Brittany and Hunter are so private and low-key, they just started telling friends recently within the last few weeks. It happened about a month ago. It was low-key and private.” Apparently Brittany’s friends “love Hunter for her” and say, “He treats her well. They are both very low-key people.”

Aaaand they’re Instagram official! Some snapshots of Hunter make their way into Brittany’s photo dump from a birthday getaway, including these sweet pics:

The Instagram love continues. Swipe for some cute snaps of Hunter (including a blink-and-you’ll miss it video of him and Brittany smooching):

A sneaky pic of Hunter is included in one of Brittany’s Instagram photo dumps:

Brittany takes to Instagram to share another photo dump, which includes several cute pics and videos of herself and Hunter. A few of my personal faves:

We’ve got ourselves a hard-launch! Brittany celebrates Hunter’s birthday with a gushing Instagram tribute, writing, “Happy birthday to my favorite person on the planet.”

She continues, “I must have done something right to deserve someone like you. Everyone who knows you, knows you are sunshine in human form. This was the first picture we ever took together (we didn’t know it was being taken) and I’ve never stopped hugging you & laughing with you since. It’s my favorite place to be. I love u @huntermoreno.”

At long last, Brittany fiiiinally confirms that she and Hunter are engaged. During an appearance on The Drew Barrymore Show ahead of The Hunting Wives season 2 premiere, Drew asks Brittany about the engagement rumors, to which she responds, “You know what? For the first time, I’m gonna tell you, I’m gonna tell you. Yes, we’re engaged, yeah.”

She adds, “I haven’t said it before. It’s okay. It’s not that big of a secret. I mean, like, who cares?”

Love this for her! Stay tuned for updates.
